# Dubbozoo — Every Unanswered Question: Evidence & Three More

**Original question set:** 18 October 2026 · **Evidence-reconciliation revision:** 18 October 2026 · **Location:** Dubbo NSW · **Source snapshot:** `5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1` · **Completed research-atlas audit of 61 historic animal records, 46 provisional species guides, 27 other Markdown files, structured data and website.**

> **Important update:** The 622 parent questions below are preserved from the original historical snapshot, but core answers now consult the 89-entry inventory, dated Taronga/Government sources and a separately graded IUCN audit. Some questions truly require animal accession records or an on-site observation. **An unverified current animal count is not the same as an unresearched conservation status.** The follow-up questions remain an independent backlog; they are not automatically answered by the parent.

> **Method:** A statement marked **unresolved** is an answered *research-status question* (the available evidence does not establish the fact), **not** a fabricated factual answer. The three follow-up questions listed under each parent are **research extensions**, not questions that have already been answered. This is an open, expanding register, not a formal zoo census. Every parent below deliberately generates exactly **three** next questions. Additional source-file question prompts and gap statements follow in later sections.

**Evidence keys:** *documented species-level* = taxon supported by research guide, not identity; *historical/partial* = dated or incomplete evidence; *unresolved* = needs new primary source; *provisional* = plausible species or location but not decisively confirmed. Current IUCN categories should be checked at the assessment level, not inferred from exhibit maps.

**Foundational sources:** [Taronga Dubbo animal directory](https://www.taronga.org.au/wildlife-and-conservation/animals/dubbo), [Taronga 2026 map](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map), [Taronga 2024–25 annual report](https://taronga.org.au/sites/default/files/2025-12/TCSA12738_Taronga_AnnualReport_2024_25_DIGITAL_FA2-compressed_compressed-min.pdf), [2025 turtle taxonomic checklist](https://iucn-tftsg.org/checklists/turtles-of-the-world-checklist-10th-edition/), [2025 IUCN giraffe taxonomy](https://iucn.org/press-release/202508/four-giraffe-species-officially-recognised-major-conservation-reclassification).

## At-a-glance source counts

- Historic inventory: **61 taxon/label leads**, not a certified complete holding register.
- `deep_guide_present` flags: **15** in inventory snapshot; newer `docs/species` drafts are distinct and require acceptance review.
- Recorded photograph observations: **6 animal groups** and **23 JPEG source copies**; public binary availability is separately recorded.
- Species-specific original questions in this atlas are followed by three follow-up questions each; automated repository prompt inventory appears in later sections.

## Africa — historic inventory research questions

### H01. Addax

**Current catalogue classification:** species lead (may now have a newer provisional draft); original evidence kind: `map`. **Photo evidence:** no user-supplied dated photograph recorded.

**Q0001. What exactly is the scientific identity of "Addax" at Dubbo?**

**Best answer — supported scientific identification:** The researched identification is **Addax nasomaculatus**. This is the species-level/label identity supported by the cited records, **not** a molecular identification of individual historic animals. See [species research](../docs/species/addax.md) and [Dubbo-specific supporting record](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map).

**Evidence:** [inventory #1](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map).

**Three next questions:**
1. Which scientific name and authority appear in a dated Taronga individual-animal or enclosure record for Addax?
2. Do any current taxonomic revisions split or combine the species/subspecies used in that record?
3. Can a keeper-supplied reference photograph or identifier confirm the exact Dubbo population's lineage?

**Q0002. What verifiable evidence establishes that Addax has been held or displayed at Dubbo?**

**Best answer — dated zoo evidence found:** **Taronga's visitor map/precinct record documents an exhibit label, but not a live inventory.** [Dubbo-specific supporting record](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map); [species research](../docs/species/addax.md). A claim of current animals or particular names requires a newer keeper confirmation.

**Evidence:** [inventory #1](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map).

**Three next questions:**
1. What is the earliest primary Dubbo holding or breeding record for Addax?
2. Which later dated Taronga documents demonstrate an actual animal rather than just a map label?
3. What is the last independently verified date that Addax was present at Dubbo, and in which facility?

**Q0003. What is the current conservation status and wild-population trend of Addax?**

**Best answer — dated scientific assessment identified:** ***Addax nasomaculatus*: Critically Endangered (CR)** in the 2016 taxon-matched IUCN assessment ([source](https://doi.org/10.2305/IUCN.UK.2016-2.RLTS.T512A50180603.en)). **Population trend:** decreasing. This is a dated assessment, **not automatically a newly reassessed 2026 figure**, and it cannot be applied to unverified subspecies. [Assessment provenance](iucn-assessment-audit-2026-10-18.md).

**Evidence:** [inventory #1](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map); [IUCN Red List](https://www.iucnredlist.org/).

**Three next questions:**
1. Which exact Red List taxon ID, assessment year and criteria apply to Addax?
2. Are the quoted figures mature individuals, total wild animals, survey detections or modeled projections?
3. How do threats and legal protections differ between the species' range countries and the Dubbo captive programme?

**Q0004. Is Addax on show now, how many are there, and can we identify any by name?**

**Best answer — current exhibit versus dated historical evidence separated:** Taronga has published a visitor-facing map or precinct reference for this animal; that is evidence of a labelled attraction, **not real-time availability**. **An October 2026 live headcount, sex/age, name and animal-by-animal ID have not been confirmed** by a dated keeper roster. [Dubbo-specific supporting record](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map); [species research](../docs/species/addax.md).

**Evidence:** [inventory #1](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map).

**Three next questions:**
1. Will the Dubbo animal team verify current individual IDs and a date-specific count for Addax?
2. Can a time-stamped observation, signage photograph and mapped viewing location independently verify today's exhibit?
3. Is Addax a public-display animal, a behind-the-scenes breeding animal, a historic resident or merely a research lead?

### H02. African elephant

**Current catalogue classification:** species lead (may now have a newer provisional draft); original evidence kind: `history`. **Photo evidence:** no user-supplied dated photograph recorded.

**Q0005. What exactly is the scientific identity of "African elephant" at Dubbo?**

**Best answer — supported scientific identification:** The researched identification is **Loxodonta africana**. This is the species-level/label identity supported by the cited records, **not** a molecular identification of individual historic animals. See [species research](../docs/species/african-elephant-historical.md) and [Dubbo-specific supporting record](https://www.animallaw.info/case/international-fund-animal-welfare-australia-pty-ltd-and-minister-environment-and-heritage).

**Evidence:** [inventory #2](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/media-releases/zoo-mourns-passing-of-last-african-elephant-in-australia).

**Three next questions:**
1. Which scientific name and authority appear in a dated Taronga individual-animal or enclosure record for African elephant?
2. Do any current taxonomic revisions split or combine the species/subspecies used in that record?
3. Can a keeper-supplied reference photograph or identifier confirm the exact Dubbo population's lineage?

**Q0006. What verifiable evidence establishes that African elephant has been held or displayed at Dubbo?**

**Best answer — dated zoo evidence found:** **An original historical publication or zoo program places the animal at Dubbo at that date.** [Dubbo-specific supporting record](https://www.animallaw.info/case/international-fund-animal-welfare-australia-pty-ltd-and-minister-environment-and-heritage); [species research](../docs/species/african-elephant-historical.md). A claim of current animals or particular names requires a newer keeper confirmation.

**Evidence:** [inventory #2](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/media-releases/zoo-mourns-passing-of-last-african-elephant-in-australia).

**Three next questions:**
1. What is the earliest primary Dubbo holding or breeding record for African elephant?
2. Which later dated Taronga documents demonstrate an actual animal rather than just a map label?
3. What is the last independently verified date that African elephant was present at Dubbo, and in which facility?

**Q0007. What is the current conservation status and wild-population trend of African elephant?**

**Best answer — answered from taxon-matched IUCN assessments:** The historic Dubbo animals were **African savanna elephants (*Loxodonta africana*)**. The [amended IUCN 2022 assessment](https://doi.org/10.2305/IUCN.UK.2022-2.RLTS.T181008073A223031019.en), assessed 13 November 2020, classifies them as **Endangered (EN), global trend decreasing**, with roughly **60% long-term decline** in the assessment. The separate **African forest elephant (*L. cyclotis*)** is **Critically Endangered, declining** ([IUCN 2021](https://doi.org/10.2305/IUCN.UK.2021-1.RLTS.T181007989A181019888.en)). Neither species' assessment provides an unambiguous current single-species wild headcount; the 2016 ~415,000 estimate combines the two, and the [2025 improved forest estimates](https://iucn.org/press-release/202511/dna-methods-help-uncover-further-african-forest-elephants-species-remains) should not be interpreted as proven population growth.

**Evidence:** [inventory #2](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/media-releases/zoo-mourns-passing-of-last-african-elephant-in-australia); [IUCN Red List](https://www.iucnredlist.org/).

**Three next questions:**
1. Which exact Red List taxon ID, assessment year and criteria apply to African elephant?
2. Are the quoted figures mature individuals, total wild animals, survey detections or modeled projections?
3. How do threats and legal protections differ between the species' range countries and the Dubbo captive programme?

**Q0008. Is African elephant on show now, how many are there, and can we identify any by name?**

**Best answer — current exhibit versus dated historical evidence separated:** **No African elephants are currently held at Dubbo.** The zoo's last African elephant, Cuddles, died in **March 2017**; this is a **former programme**, distinct from the Asian elephants. [ABC contemporaneous account](https://www.abc.net.au/news/2017-03-22/last-african-elephant-in-australia-dies-at-dubbo-zoo/8377038). The six historically named elephants are not a live headcount.

**Evidence:** [inventory #2](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/media-releases/zoo-mourns-passing-of-last-african-elephant-in-australia).

**Three next questions:**
1. Will the Dubbo animal team verify current individual IDs and a date-specific count for African elephant?
2. Can a time-stamped observation, signage photograph and mapped viewing location independently verify today's exhibit?
3. Is African elephant a public-display animal, a behind-the-scenes breeding animal, a historic resident or merely a research lead?

**Q0239. What is the current conservation status and wild-population trend of Domestic pygmy goat?**

**Best answer — answered — domestic animal not evaluated as a wild taxon:** **No applicable single IUCN wild-population trend for this domestic form.** The animal is **Capra hircus (domestic pygmy goat, not independent wild species)**; applying the threatened status of a related wild species (for example wild Bactrian camel or wild water buffalo) would be taxonomically incorrect. [species research](../docs/species/domestic-pygmy-goat.md).

**Evidence:** [inventory #60](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json), [source](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map); [IUCN Red List](https://www.iucnredlist.org/).

### H61. European fallow deer

**Current catalogue classification:** species lead (may now have a newer provisional draft); original evidence kind: `precinct`. **Photo evidence:** no user-supplied dated photograph recorded.

This is the species-level/label identity supported by the cited records, **not** a molecular identification of individual historic animals. See [species research](../docs/species/european-fallow-deer.md) and [Dubbo-specific supporting record](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/precincts-and-pitstops/wild-herds).

## Repo-wide uncertainties, contradictions and source-verification questions

**Q0245. How many animal taxa have ever been at Dubbo zoo, precisely?**

**Best answer — answered by expanded inventory evidence:** **Not precisely known.** The [current historical register](all-time-animal-inventory.md) contains **89 researched entries**, including provisional visitor sightings, temporary veterinary patients and zoo conservation animals. It is **not 89 verified distinct biological species**. A definitive 1977–2026 total needs Taronga's year-by-year zoo-specific accession/transfer ledger, reconciling synonyms and animal categories.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Can Taronga provide its accession/deaccession register from 1977 to 2026?
2. Which reported taxa are Sydney-only, shared conservation projects or secondary-list errors?
3. How do we distinguish an individual, species, subspecies and generic exhibit label in a final canonical list?

**Q0246. Do the 61 catalogue entries represent 61 species?**

**Best answer — answered — earlier assumption obsolete:** **No.** The original 61-entry catalogue was a working name list, not a certified species count. Research now contains **89 entries**; taxon ambiguities, domesticated breeds, free-ranging animals and historical unverified accessions mean no naïve entry count equals the number of distinct zoo-kept species. [Inventory](all-time-animal-inventory.md).

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Which checklist entries refer to species complexes?
2. Which aliases are duplicates for a single taxon?
3. Should historical displays, closed reserves and transient animals appear in separate counts?

**Q0247. Is the repo's number of guides consistent with its historical status flags?**

**Best answer — verified current repository counts:** **Yes for guide-path coverage, not for historical completeness.** The revised [inventory JSON](../data/all-time-animal-inventory.json) lists **89 unique entries with 89 guide paths**. Its evidence flags distinguish **14 formal-holding questions**, including 11 new historic visitor/plan leads, and **six exact taxon questions**. That does not prove all 89 were official zoo collection animals.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Which newer drafts meet the project's depth-and-citation acceptance checklist?
2. Which draft guide statuses can be promoted with source-by-source verification?
3. Can a CI check reconcile directory contents against the JSON catalogue without overstating research quality?

**Q0248. Are all 23 catalogue photographs available as public image binaries?**

**Best answer — not proven — repository evidence only:** **No.** A photograph inventory or SHA-256 manifest is **not** the same as publicly committed photo JPEG binaries. The repository's personal photos should only be listed as publicly available after checking exact paths and rights/visitor privacy. [Photo register](../photos/README.md).

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Who owns copyright and authorises public use of each JPEG?
2. Which faces, number plates, children or private details require blur/crop before publication?
3. Will the gallery display separately hashed privacy-safe derivatives linked back to private original manifests?

**Q0249. Can the repo establish when and where each photograph was taken?**

**Best answer — verified data limitation:** No. The supplied images generally lack retained capture-time/GPS EXIF. The message-submission date is not a camera timestamp; context-based venue hypotheses are not GPS proof.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Can the camera originals be recovered with timestamps and location EXIF?
2. Do the user's diary, tickets, Google Timeline or GPX traces corroborate a particular visit date?
3. Can provenance confidence be stated per photograph rather than copied from a whole conversation?

**Q0250. Can we identify named giraffes by coat pattern alone?**

**Best answer — photo identity not proven:** **No.** Species/animal-type observation and named individual identity are different evidence standards. The available personal photographs lack keeper-verified date/lineage/name matching, so coat pattern, a public sign or a keeper talk does not prove which named individual appears. An original timestamp plus keeper photo/ID record is needed.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Does the zoo keep a date-stamped individual giraffe coat-pattern catalogue?
2. Which photographic regions give robust longitudinal matches as animals age?
3. Can observer confidence be tested against known labeled individual comparisons?

**Q0251. Is the 2025 four-species giraffe classification interchangeable with older Vulnerable status?**

**Best answer — answered — taxonomy must match status:** **No.** Older IUCN giraffe assessments sometimes considered giraffe a single species; the 2025 four-species recognition changes which biological unit an assessment refers to. A generic historic **Vulnerable** label cannot simply be copied to an unidentified captive lineage. Check the documented 2025 taxonomic species and the corresponding IUCN Red List assessment year. [Giraffe guide](giraffe.md).

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. When will each newly accepted giraffe species receive its next category assessment?
2. What exact giraffe lineage is in Taronga Dubbo's herd according to breeding records?
3. Can historic population trend reports be harmonised across taxonomy revisions?

**Best answer — historical only:** No. It is a **dated April 2025 Taronga report**, not a live headcount or proof of the animals in the photos.

**Q0257. Which siamang map grid is correct: 12Q or 10G?**

**Best answer — open — two dated maps conflict:** Taronga's official references give different siamang map grids (12Q and 10G). **Neither can be proved canonical for an October 2026 visit** from those pages alone; read the map edition/date and the currently posted entrance map. The species itself is documented at [Siamang Islands](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/posts/iconic-species-returns-to-taronga-western-plains-zoo), independently of the grid conflict.

**Q0258. Does 65 at Platypus Rescue HQ mean 65 resident platypuses?**

**Best answer — answered — capacity is not occupancy:** **No.** Taronga's Platypus Rescue HQ design capacity for **up to 65 platypuses** is a facility limit, **not the number rescued, currently housed or on public view**. Actual intake and resident headcounts require dated zoo rescue/keeper records. [Platypus research](platypus.md).

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Q0259. Is the platypus nationally listed as Vulnerable in Australia in 2026?**

**Best answer — legal listing distinguished from nomination:** A species being proposed for threatened-species listing or subject to assessment **is not identical to a final EPBC Act listing instrument**. Check the official 2026 Australian Government legislative/species profile and effective date before saying platypus is nationally Vulnerable; IUCN's global category is a **separate framework**. [Platypus evidence review](platypus-2026-evidence-review.md).

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. What detailed evidence was considered or excluded in the non-prioritisation?
2. Which revised estimates could support a future EPBC assessment?
3. Do the 2026 state distributions align with model-based decline scenarios?

**Q0260. Are Taronga's $12.1m and $12.5m platypus project costs reconciled?**

**Best answer — answered at source level; accounting reconciliation open:** Taronga's [19 February 2025 one-year article](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/posts/platypus-rescue-hq-turns-1) states **$12.5 million**, while its [8 October 2025 facility explanation](https://cdn.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/posts/protecting-the-platypus) states **$12.1 million in funding**. These are **different published statements**, but there is no itemised capital-budget reconciliation supporting a conclusion that one is wrong or why the scope differs.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Which contracts and budget categories are included in each published amount?
2. Which figure is in the relevant audited annual report and what date does it cover?
3. Has either agency published an explicit correction or budget reconciliation?

**Q0261. Has the zoo successfully bred platypuses in its new centre?**

**Best answer — answered — successful Dubbo breeding has not been documented:** Taronga's [12 February 2025 announcement](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/posts/taronga-collaborates-with-international-partners-to-conserve-the-iconic-platypus) reports **four adult platypuses forming the first research breeding population** at Dubbo. It does **not announce an offspring born there**. The documented **2024 breeding success** was in the **Royal National Park**, following a NSW translocation, not at Dubbo Rescue HQ ([Taronga source](https://www.taronga.org.au/about/newsroom/posts/first-ever-platypus-translocation-in-nsw-results-in-breeding-success)). Therefore **no Dubbo HQ breeding success is established by these sources**.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Has Taronga released a dated birth notice or scientific breeding report?
2. What scientifically documented reproductive milestones have been achieved?
3. Are any unpublished results confidential and therefore inappropriate for public release?

**Q0262. Does the 2026 Royal National Park platypus population result describe Dubbo?**

**Best answer — verified location distinction:** No. That report concerns a Sydney-area reintroduction; Dubbo is the site of a separate rescue/research centre. The locations, project objectives and counts should not be conflated.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Q0263. Are exact sex, age, names and behavioural meanings recoverable from the zoo photos?**

**Best answer — verified limitation:** Not reliably. An image documents visible morphology and actions but cannot on its own establish most identity and welfare attributes. Confirm them from keepers and directly observed longitudinal evidence.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Q0264. Should the zoo's live website link be claimed tested?**

**Best answer — deployment cannot be inferred from source control:** **No.** A GitHub repository containing `index.html` does not prove its GitHub Pages/custom-domain deployment is available, returning HTTP 200, or showing the latest commit. Live deployment was **not verified** in this study; verify the actual URL, current response and rendered site before marking acceptance.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Does GitHub Pages currently have an enabled Pages URL and which branch/path serves it?
2. Does a live 200 response match the SHA and modified content of the latest commit?
3. Are all Markdown links, navigation anchors, mobile layouts and a11y behaviours validated?

**Q0265. Can the inventory's secondary-only animal leads be counted as confirmed Dubbo holdings?**

**Best answer — resolved evidence-grade distinction:** **No.** A species mentioned in a visitor map, broad Taronga-wide report or observer recollection is **not automatically a formally held Dubbo animal**. The [89-entry source-graded inventory](all-time-animal-inventory.md) distinguishes first-party records, temporary hospital cases, wild/free-ranging visitors and **14 accession/arrival questions**.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Which primary stock-transfer or animal-record tables explicitly identify Dubbo?
2. Do archive photos and exhibit plans support any otherwise-secondary taxon?
3. Should unsupported leads be removed or flagged separately from the verified historical list?

**Q0266. Is 'Least Concern' a guarantee that a zoo animal or local wild population faces no risk?**

**Best answer — verified principle:** No. IUCN status is a population-scale category, not a diagnosis of an individual or assurance that every local population is stable.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Which locality-specific threats differ from global conservation status?
2. How old is the global assessment and are there threatened local subspecies?
3. What zoo and wild conservation activities are independently documented?

**Q0267. Can education prompts in guides be treated as observed behaviour?**

**Best answer — verified interpretation:** No. Questions such as 'can you see it feeding?' are prompts for **future observation**, not claims that any recorded animal was feeding at the moment of a still photograph.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. Which prompts need a recorded observation period to answer?
2. Can an observation form capture start/end time and evidence source per answer?
3. Which questions require audio, video or a qualified keeper rather than a still image?

**Q0268. What is the difference between a species checklist and a complete individual-animal register?**

**Best answer — verified definition:** The checklist tracks taxa or labels and sources; a register tracks individual animals, accession dates, movements, births, deaths, and identity. The latter is not publicly available in this project.

**Evidence:** [project README](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/README.md), [research method](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/research-method.md) and links in the relevant species guides.

**Three next questions:**
1. What fields would a consent-respecting, date-scoped accession register require?
2. Who is authorised to release relevant husbandry records?
3. How should uncertain species aliases and duplicate individuals be reconciled?

### docs/african-wild-dog.md

5 literal questions; 2 explicit evidence-gap lines.

**Q0278. What resolves this explicit evidence gap in # African wild dog — Lycaon pictus: "The IUCN notes that some apparent population increases since older assessments are from discovering previously unknown packs rather than verified recovery. [IUCN assessment][iucn]"?**

**Best answer — answered from IUCN:** The [2025 *Lycaon pictus* IUCN assessment](https://doi.org/10.2305/IUCN.UK.2025-2.RLTS.T12436A221299510.en) treats previously unknown packs newly **detected** as a survey/knowledge increase, which cannot automatically be interpreted as new individuals born or an increasing true population. The global Red List trend is **decreasing**; to show recovery one would need consistent repeated territory surveys, reproduction and survival estimates.

**Source file:** [`docs/african-wild-dog.md`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/african-wild-dog.md).

**Q0290. What is the difference between a social family group and an animal's individual identity?**

**Best answer — answered — biology and identification:** An elephant **family group** is a social arrangement of usually related females and dependent young, potentially involving other associates; **individual identity** means distinguishing one known elephant from another by keeper records, distinctive markings and provenance. A visible group does not tell us each elephant's name, pedigree, age or hierarchy.

**Source file:** [`docs/asian-elephant.md`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/asian-elephant.md).

**Q0293. What resolves this explicit evidence gap in # Asian elephant — Dubbo Zoo deep research guide: "Sexual differences are nuanced: Asian females seldom develop externally large tusks; males may also be tuskless. Relative body size and tusks alone cannot confirm the sexes of these two"?**

**Best answer — answered — sex cannot be inferred from tusks alone:** Asian elephants show substantial sex variation, but **many females lack large visible tusks and males may be tuskless (makhnas)**. Body size, tusk presence or a single viewing angle is insufficient for a definitive individual sex assignment. Use the animal's keeper record or confident reproductive/genetic evidence. [Elephant guide](asian-elephant.md).

**Q0306. What fresh-water resources must be conserved to support wild hippos and other species?**

**Best answer — answered — ecological requirements:** Healthy **year-round deep pools and river channels** for daytime immersion, connected wetland and grassland feeding margins, dry-season refuges and unobstructed access between water and grazing areas support wild hippos. Conserving flow, limiting extraction/pollution and protecting riparian cover help hippos and freshwater communities; this is a **general ecological answer**, not an assessment of Dubbo enclosure water quality.

**Source file:** [`docs/common-hippopotamus.md`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/common-hippopotamus.md).

**Best answer — answered — genetic origin not inferable from images:** A photographed Galápagos giant tortoise can be classified **Chelonoidis (Galápagos complex)**, but **not** assigned an island species, precise IUCN status or original captive ancestry from a generic exhibit sign or shell shape. ### docs/galapagos-giant-tortoise.md

**Q0313. What resolves this explicit evidence gap in # Galápagos giant tortoises at Dubbo — evidence-led field guide: "Identity: Galápagos giant tortoise, Chelonoidis Galápagos complex, exact island lineage/accepted rank unverified."?**

**Best answer — answered — study needs species-level lineage:** **Chelonoidis spp.** is the supported Galápagos giant-tortoise complex identification. Island-specific taxa differ in conservation status. Only accession/studbook origin, breeding record or genetic study can identify island lineage; without that, the scientific label should remain **Chelonoidis sp.**, not an invented one.

**Source file:** [`docs/galapagos-giant-tortoise.md`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/galapagos-giant-tortoise.md).

**Q0342. What is gained by moving as a herd and what can it cost when pasture is scarce?**

**Best answer — answered — herd benefits and costs:** Moving in a zebra herd can improve **predator detection, access to social information and coordinated travel**, but if pasture is scarce it can also mean **greater competition, trampling and disease/parasite exposure**. These are ecology principles; they do not prove herd behaviour at a particular Dubbo visit.

**Best answer — answered — practical children-safe river care:** With adult supervision, children can **put litter in bins, join organised supervised clean-ups on safe banks, avoid balloons and loose fishing line, refill water bottles and prevent plastic entering stormwater drains**. Adults should handle sharp hooks, fishing line entangling wildlife, needles and unsafe river edges. Do **not** enter fast water, touch injured animals or remove aquatic habitat.

**Q0352. How might an upstream barrier alter a platypus population even when a waterhole remains?**

**Best answer — answered — population connectivity mechanism:** An upstream dam, weir or habitat barrier can **fragment breeding populations, restrict dispersal and gene flow, change stream temperatures/flow, alter aquatic prey and block movement to refuge pools**. A pool may still hold water while the wider platypus population loses connectivity; demonstrate local impact with water-quality and population surveys.

**Evidence:** [`docs/platypus.md`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/platypus.md).

**Best answer — answer — concrete proof required identified:** The exact missing evidence is an **original rights-cleared camera file** (rather than checksum or chat-upload date), a usable capture timestamp/location if available, and for individual animal names or sex **a keeper-matched ID record for that date**. A visible visitor requires privacy/consent review. Without those files, keep photo-specific unknowns explicit. [Photo manifest](../photos/README.md).

**Evidence:** [`photos/README.md`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/photos/README.md).

**Three follow-up questions:**
1. Can a verified time-stamped camera original or keeper identification settle this question for # Submitted photographs — integrity and publication status?
2. What alternative viewing-angle or animal-identity explanation must be ruled out?
3. Which private metadata can verify the answer without unnecessarily publishing visitors or children?

## Catalogue/research status reconciliation: 25 pending map labels

A pending map label does not mean there is no text file about the species: `docs/species/` contains many newer drafts, but the canonical catalogue has not independently promoted them to deep/reviewed status. Each entry must clear substantive evidence criteria rather than being automatically ticked merely because a filename exists.

**Q0572. Why is Addax still flagged as a map-label research lead, and what would make its guide accepted?**

**Best answer — guide completed; map-only research label obsolete:** The old map-label backlog is **outdated**: [Addax](../docs/species/addax.md) now has a dedicated zoo/ecology research profile. Its zoo-specific supporting record is [here](https://www.taronga.org.au/dubbo-zoo/map). **Guide exists** does not certify living 2026 animal numbers, and some exact taxonomic or historic individual questions remain. Any claim the guide is still unwritten should be removed from the active backlog.

**Evidence:** [`data/species-catalogue.json`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/species-catalogue.json) · [`docs/coverage-audit.md`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/coverage-audit.md).

**Three further questions:**
1. What first-party source document or machine-readable schema test would settle this for Addax?
2. How might differing versions of taxonomy, zoo maps or conservation lists affect this answer?
3. What reproducible check or documented acceptance criterion would prevent this uncertainty recurring?

## Website, engineering, evidence provenance and scientific-method questions

**Q0597. Does the GitHub repository prove the public Dubbozoo website is live?**

**Best answer — deployment cannot be inferred from source control:** **No.** A GitHub repository containing `index.html` does not prove its GitHub Pages/custom-domain deployment is available, returning HTTP 200, or showing the latest commit. Live deployment was **not verified** in this study; verify the actual URL, current response and rendered site before marking acceptance.

**Evidence:** [`index.html`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/index.html) · [`data/all-time-animal-inventory.json`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/all-time-animal-inventory.json) · [`data/species-catalogue.json`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/species-catalogue.json).

**Q0600. Do the 61 historical animals establish all species ever at the zoo?**

**Best answer — answered — correct denominator and scope:** The old **61** and newer **89** are **research catalogue entries**, not independent biological species verified to have been kept in the zoo. [Historical methodology](all-time-animal-inventory.md).

## Dedicated Platypus Rescue HQ JSON: ten evidence reconciliation questions

**Source:** [`data/platypus.json`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/platypus.json). This file separately describes a conservation facility and legal-status context; it is not a seventh photo observation group.

**Q0613. Does the platypus research JSON contain a verified Josh photograph?**

**Best answer — verified limitation or cited historical fact:** No. The fields `user_observation_verified=false`, `user_photo_count=0`, and the empty photos list explicitly mean it is a species research entry, not a claimed first-hand observation.

**Evidence:** [`data/platypus.json`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/platypus.json), [platypus source reconciliation](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/platypus-2026-evidence-review.md).

**Three further questions:**
1. Which original Taronga or government record supplies the decisive date-specific evidence for this platypus question?
2. How does the time, location and population metric differ from a superficially similar cited platypus statistic?
3. What evidence or independent check should be added to the JSON before this claim is promoted from historical context to current certainty?

**Q0614. Is the Galápagos tortoise or platypus research marked as the same record type as photographic observations?**

**Best answer — verified limitation or cited historical fact:** No. The platypus JSON has a dedicated research-only entry; the other animals have photo observation records. A researched species is not automatically an observed animal.

**Evidence:** [`data/platypus.json`](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/data/platypus.json), [platypus source reconciliation](https://github.com/joshualparris/Dubbozoo/blob/5e08328fcdd639f6fd9cbcf872842dcd5efa08d1/docs/platypus-2026-evidence-review.md).

**Best answer — verified limitation or cited historical fact:** No. `21M` is a visitor map grid reference; the evidence field `gps=null` does not contain the user's camera location.

**Best answer — answered — capacity is not occupancy:** **No.** Taronga's Platypus Rescue HQ design capacity for **up to 65 platypuses** is a facility limit, **not the number rescued, currently housed or on public view**. Actual intake and resident headcounts require dated zoo rescue/keeper records. [Platypus research](platypus.md).

**Q0619. Is the 2026 federal platypus nomination equivalent to official Vulnerable listing under the EPBC Act?**

**Best answer — legal listing distinguished from nomination:** A species being proposed for threatened-species listing or subject to assessment **is not identical to a final EPBC Act listing instrument**. Check the official 2026 Australian Government legislative/species profile and effective date before saying platypus is nationally Vulnerable; IUCN's global category is a **separate framework**.
